# Commissions
Aliases: Comission, Comissions, Commisions, Commission
Categories: Confirmations needed, Dwarven Mines, Quests
[Commissions]
Commissions are [[Mining]] quests issued by the [[King]] and his [[Emissaries]] that involve completing a certain task repeatedly in either the [[Dwarven Mines]] or [[Crystal Hollows]]. Completing a Commission rewards the player with [[Mining|Mining XP]], [[Heart of the Mountain]] XP and either ᠅ [[Mithril Powder]], ᠅ [[Gemstone Powder]] or ᠅ [[Glacite Powder]].
Completing Commisions contributes to the tiered [[Achievements|"Hard Working Miner" Achievement]].
## Mechanics
Initially, players have only two commissions slots, but after completing 100 commissions, players will gain a third commission slot. Additionally, at [[Heart of the Mountain|Heart of the Mountain V (5)]], players can unlock the [[Heart of the Mountain|Core of the Mountain]] perk for a fourth slot. Commission slots 1 & 3, and 2 & 4 have the same quest pool and cannot have duplicate types of commissions.
[[Crystal Hollows]] commissions are a different set of 4 commissions. They all draw from the same Commission pool, and also cannot have duplicates.
### Rewards
||[[Dwarven Mines]]|[[Crystal Hollows]]|[[Glacite Tunnels]]|
|-|-|-|-|
|Mining XP|5-10k|10-20k|12.5-25k|
|HotM XP|100 at Heart of the Mountain 1; 200 at Heart of the Mountain 2; 400 at Heart of the Mountain 3+; 0 at Heart of the Mountain 10|400|750|
|Powder|500 ᠅ [[Mithril Powder]]; 1,000 ᠅ [[Mithril Powder]] at HotM 10.|1,000 ᠅ [[Gemstone Powder]]; 2,000 ᠅ [[Gemstone Powder]] at HotM 10.|1,500 ᠅ [[Glacite Powder]]; 3,000 ᠅ [[Glacite Powder]] at HotM 10.|
#### Daily Rewards
First 4 commissions every day wherever they are give 900 more HoTM XP until it is maxed. Once Heart of the Mountain is maxed, daily commissions give an additional 1600 ᠅ [[Mithril Powder]], 3200 ᠅ [[Gemstone Powder]] or 4000 ᠅ [[Glacite Powder]]. Daily bonuses are reset at midnight Hypixel Server Time.
### Milestones
Completing a certain number of commissions rewards the player with unique items and abilities.
|Milestone|Commissions|Rewards|
|-|-|-|
|Ⅰ|5|[[Emissaries]] 1x [[Royal Compass]] +100,000 Mining Experience ([[Mining]]) +20 XP ([[SkyBlock Levels]])|
|Ⅱ|25|+200,000 Mining Experience ([[Mining]]) +30 XP ([[SkyBlock Levels]])|
|Ⅲ|100|+1 Commission Slot +400,000 Mining Experience ([[Mining]]) +30 XP ([[SkyBlock Levels]])|
|Ⅳ|250|1x [[Travel Scroll to Dwarven Mines]] +800,000 Mining Experience ([[Mining]]) +50 XP ([[SkyBlock Levels]])|
|Ⅴ|500|1x [[Royal Pigeon]] +1,200,000 Mining Experience ([[Mining]]) +50 XP ([[SkyBlock Levels]])|
|Ⅵ|750|1x [[Travel Scroll to the Crystal Nucleus]] +1,600,000 Mining Experience ([[Mining]]) +75 XP ([[SkyBlock Levels]])|
## Commissions
### Dwarven Mines Commissions
Different Commissions are selected depending on which slot is selected. These Commissions can only be completed in the [[Dwarven Mines]].
Certain Commissions require the player to mine ores in a certain area of the [[Dwarven Mines]]. This [[Locations|area]] is picked at random from the following locations:
- [[Cliffside Veins]]
- [[Lava Springs]]
- [[Rampart's Quarry]]
- [[Royal Mines]]
- [[Upper Mines]]
Players can have multiple of those at the same times, they're listed as one in the interest of brevity.
Commissions belong to one of two groups Event and Non-event. Players will always have at least 1 of each group, regardless of how many slots they have.
|Name|Description|Group|
|-|-|-|
|Glacite Walker Slayer|Slay 50 Glacite Walkers ([[Glacite Walker]]).|Non-event|
|Goblin Slayer|Slay 100 Goblins ([[Goblin]]).|Non-event|
|Mithril Miner|Mine 350 Mithril Ore ([[Mithril]]).|Non-event|
|[Area] Mithril|Mine 250 Mithril Ore ([[Mithril]]) in [area].|Non-event|
|Titanium Miner|Mine 15 [[Titanium]].|Non-event|
|[Area] Titanium|Mine 10 Titanium Ore in [area].|Non-event|
|Treasure Hoarder Puncher|Damage Treasure Hoarders ([[Treasure Hoarder]]) 10 times.|Non-event|
|Goblin Raid|Participate in the [[Goblin Raid]] event.|Event|
|Goblin Raid Slayer|Slay 20 Goblins during the [[Goblin Raid]] Event.|Event|
|Elusive Goblin Slayer|Slay 1 [[Golden Goblin]] or [[Diamond Goblin]].|Event|
|Raffle|Participate in the [[Raffle]] Event.|Event|
|Lucky Raffle|Deposit 20 Raffle Tickets ([[Raffle Ticket]]) during the [[Raffle]] Event.|Event|
|Star Sentry Puncher|Punch Star Sentries ([[Star Sentry]]) 10 times.|Non-event|
|2x Mithril Powder Collector|Collect 500 ᠅ [[Mithril Powder]] during the [[2x Powder]] event.|Event|
##### Starter
The player's first few commissions are slightly altered to make them easier.
|Name|Description|
|-|-|
|First Event|Participate in any Mining Event.|
|Mithril Miner|Mine 50/150 [[Mithril]] anywhere.|
|Titanium Miner|Mine 2 [[Titanium]] anywhere.|
|Mines Slayer|Kill 50 Creatures in the Mines.|
For the First Event commission, [[Dwarven Mines|Passive Events]] count too.
The Mithril Miner commission will be 50 the first time, and 150 the second time, before becoming the usual 350.
### Crystal Hollows Commissions
These Commissions can only be completed in the [[Crystal Hollows]].
Certain Commissions require the player to collect certain [[Gemstone|Gemstones]]. This [[Gemstone]] is picked at random from the following ores:
- [[Gemstone|Amber]]
- [[Gemstone|Amethyst]]
- [[Gemstone|Jade]]
- [[Gemstone|Ruby]]
- [[Gemstone|Sapphire]]
- [[Gemstone|Topaz]]
Players can have multiple of those at the same times, they're listed as one in the interest of brevity.
Certain Commissions require the player to obtain a certain [[Gemstone Crystals|Gemstone Crystal]]. This [[Gemstone Crystals|crystal]] is picked at random from the following crystals:
- [[Gemstone|Amber]]
- [[Gemstone|Amethyst]]
- [[Gemstone|Jade]]
- [[Gemstone|Sapphire]]
- [[Gemstone|Topaz]]
Players can have multiple of those at the same times, they're listed as one in the interest of brevity.
Commissions belong to one of two groups Crystal and Non-crystal. Players will always have at least 1 of each group, regardless of how many slots they have.
|Commission Name|Commission Description|Group|
|-|-|-|
|[Type] Gemstone Collector|Collect 1,000 [type] Gemstones ([[Gemstone]]).|Non-crystal|
|Automaton Slayer|Slay 13 Automatons ([[Automaton]]) in the Precursor Remnant.|Non-crystal|
|Sludge Slayer|Slay 25 Sludges ([[Sludge]]) in the Jungle.|Non-crystal|
|Team Treasurite Member Slayer|Slay 13 Team Treasurite Members ([[Team Treasurite]]) in the Mithril Deposits.|Non-crystal|
|Goblin Slayer|Slay 13 Goblins ([[Goblin (disambiguation)]]) in the Goblin Holdout.|Non-crystal|
|Yog Slayer|Slay 13 Yogs ([[Yog]]) in the Magma Fields.|Non-crystal|
|Chest Looter|Open 3 chests.|Non-crystal|
|Thyst Slayer|Slay 5 Thysts ([[Thyst]]) in the Jungle.|Non-crystal|
|Hard Stone Miner|Mine 1,000 [[Hard Stone]].|Non-crystal|
|[Gemstone] Crystal Hunter|Find a [gemstone] Crystal ([[Gemstone Crystals]]) in the [area].|Crystal|
|Boss Corleone Slayer|Slay 1 [[Boss Corleone]] in the Mithril Deposits.|Crystal|
### Glacite Tunnels
These Commissions can only be completed in the [[Glacite Tunnels]].
Certain Commissions require the player to collect certain [[Gemstone|Gemstones]] or [[Dwarven Metals]]. This is picked at random from the following:
- [[Gemstone|Aquamarine]]
- [[Gemstone|Citrine]]
- [[Glacite]]
- [[Onyx (disambiguation)|Onyx]]
- [[Gemstone|Peridot]]
- [[Tungsten]]
- [[Umber]]
Players can have multiple of this kind of commission. They're listed as one in the interest of brevity.
Commissions belong to one of two groups Mineshaft and Non-mineshaft. Players will always have at least 1 of each group, regardless of how many slots they have.
|Commission Name|Commission Description|Group|
|-|-|-|
|[Material] Collector|Collect 1,500 [Material].|Non-mineshaft|
|Mineshaft Explorer|Discover 1 Mineshaft.|Mineshaft|
|Corpse Looter|Loot 2 [[Frozen Corpses]].|Mineshaft|
|Scrap Collector|Obtain 1 [[Suspicious Scrap]].|Mineshaft|
|Maniac Slayer|Slay 10 Maniacs ([[Glacite Maniacs]]) in the [[Glacite Mineshafts]].|Mineshaft|
### Removed
|Name|Description|
|-|-|
|Powder Ghast Puncher|Damage a [[Powder Ghast]] 5 times.|
## Notes
- While types of commission are no longer locked to a specific slot (for example 1st and 3rd), they are set in a way where the player will not have 4 commissions that depend on Events, obtaining Crystals or being in [[Glacite Tunnels]].
- [[Compact]] and [[Heart of the Mountain|Efficient Miner]] perk work for speeding up commissions and calculates the total raw material amount.
- [[Pristine]] works for speeding up commissions but only counting the Flawed Gemstone drops as individual drops rather than the raw x80 per Flawed.
## Trivia
- While the [[Mining|Mining XP]] gained from milestones is not boosted by any experience multipliers, the one obtained from Commissions themselves is. This makes them an effective way to level pets, especially within the [[Glacite Tunnels]] where all commissions can be done near one another and require no prep time.
- If you kill a large [[Sludge]] which then splits into smaller Sludges, the large one and the small ones all count as individual kills for the Sludge Slayer commission.

# Copper
Aliases: Cooper
Categories: Confirmations needed, Currencies
[Copper]
Type: [[Currency]]
Copper is a [[Currency]] used within [[The Garden]]. It can be used to purchase various items and upgrades from [[The Garden|The Desk]], and upgrading the [[Composter]].
Copper is co-op soulbound and cannot be traded between players. However, items purchased with Copper are tradable.
## Display
All [[Player|Players]] have their Copper displayed on the right side of the screen in the scoreboard while on [[The Garden]].
## Obtaining
Varying amounts of Copper can be obtained by completing offers from [[The Garden|Visitors]] in [[The Garden]].
Copper can also be obtained from analyzing [[Mutations]] with the [[Crop Analyzer]].
Copper reward is determined based on the [[The Garden|amount of items requested]] and the Visitor's rarity, with this formula: Copper=BaseItems2000ItemQuantityMultiplier×VisitorRarityMultiplier×1.3 where (BaseItems and ItemQuantityMultiplier are from the [[The Garden|Requests]] formula) VisitorRarityMultiplier is determined by the visitor's rarity:
- UNCOMMON: ×1
- RARE: ×1
- LEGENDARY: ×5
- MYTHIC: ×5[Confirm (Confirmation needed; When this info is confirmed, remove this.)]
- SPECIAL: ×5

|-|-|-|
|[[Rose Dragon Pet]]|[[Rose Dragon Pet]]|+10.2-20% Copper|
|[[Garden Chips]]|Synthesis Chip ([[Garden Chips]])|+1-40% Copper from the [[Crop Analyzer]]|
|[[Finnegan]]|[[Finnegan]]|+10% Copper from Visitors ([[Garden Visitors]])|
|[[Atmospheric Filter]]|[[Atmospheric Filter]]|+5% Copper from Visitors ([[Garden Visitors]]) during Winter|
|[[Ladybug Shard]]|[[Ladybug Shard]]|+1-10% Copper from Visitors ([[Garden Visitors]])|

### Crop Upgrades
Crop Upgrades increase the player's [[Farming Fortune]] while harvesting certain crops. Each crop can be upgraded at the Desk for Copper, and each upgrade grants +5 [[Farming Fortune]]. In total 99,905 Copper is needed to max out upgrades for all 13 crops.
|Level|Requirement|Cost|Cumulative Cost|Cumulative Frmng Frtne ([[Farming Fortune]])|
|-|-|-|-|-|
|Tier I|Garden II (2) ([[The Garden]])|5 Copper|5 Copper|+5 [[Farming Fortune]]|
|Tier II|Garden IV (4) ([[The Garden]])|10 Copper|15 Copper|+10 [[Farming Fortune]]|
|Tier III|Garden VI (6) ([[The Garden]])|20 Copper|35 Copper|+15 [[Farming Fortune]]|
|Tier IV|Garden VIII (8) ([[The Garden]])|50 Copper|85 Copper|+20 [[Farming Fortune]]|

|Tier VII|Garden XIII (13) ([[The Garden]])|1,000 Copper|1,685 Copper|+35 [[Farming Fortune]]|
|Tier VIII|Garden XIV (14) ([[The Garden]])|2,000 Copper|3,685 Copper|+40 [[Farming Fortune]]|
|Tier IX|Garden XV (15) ([[The Garden]])|4,000 Copper|7,685 Copper|+45 [[Farming Fortune]]|
### Composter Upgrades
Copper can be used to purchase [[Composter|Composter upgrades]], which can be used to increase [[Compost]] production efficiency. There are 5 upgrade branches: Composter Speed, Multi Drop, Fuel Cap, Organic Matter Cap and Cost Reduction. 178,250 Copper is required to max out all [[Composter]] upgrades.
|Tier|Cost|Cumulative Cost|

### Greenhouse Upgrades
Copper can be used to purchase [[Greenhouse]] upgrades, which increases [[Greenhouse]] Growth Speed or [[Greenhouse]] Plant Yield. 31,700 Copper is required to max out all [[Greenhouse]] upgrades.
|Tier|Cost|Cumulative Cost|
|-|-|-|
|1|50 Copper|50 Copper|
|2|100 Copper|150 Copper|
|3|200 Copper|350 Copper|
|4|500 Copper|850 Copper|
|5|1,000 Copper|1,850 Copper|
|6|2,000 Copper|3,850 Copper|
|7|3,000 Copper|6,850 Copper|
|8|4,000 Copper|10,850 Copper|
|9|5,000 Copper|15,850 Copper each|
## Trivia
- 286,800 Copper is needed to max out all crop, [[Composter]], and [[Greenhouse]] upgrades.
- 1 Copper is currently worth if converted to [[Dedication|Dedication I]] on the [[Bazaar]].
  - Therefore all the Copper needed for upgrades is roughly equal to .
  - For [[Green Thumb (Enchantment)|Green Thumb]], this would be

# Critters
Aliases: Critter
Categories: Confirmations needed, Mob Types
[Critters]
Type: Mob Type ([[Mob Types]])
Critters are variants of [[Mob|Mobs]] that can only be captured to grant the player an [[Attributes|Attribute Shard]] on capture.
Most Critters do not have traditional [[Health]], with a few exceptions. Critters cannot take melee or ranged damage, requiring [[Hunting Toolkit|Hunting Tools]] instead.
## Overview
Critters can be found on the [[Moonglade Marsh]], [[Torrhus Canyon]], [[Critter Safari]], and [[Lotus Atoll]]. Critters can be captured in multiple ways:
- Through the use of a [[Lassos|Lasso]] (e.g. [[Phanpyre]], [[Dreadwing]], [[Mountain Goat]])
- Through the use of a [[Fishing Nets|Fishing Net]] (e.g. [[Shellwise]], [[Seashine]], [[Timil]])
- Through the use of a [[Critter Capsule]] (e.g. [[Cavernfish]], [[Bluebird]], [[Scrappy]])
- Through feeding certain items (e.g. [[Mochibear]], [[Tewtil]])
- Through other various ways (e.g. [[Hideonleaf]], [[Lotum]], [[Hideyho]])
## List of Critters
While these mobs technically count as Critters, [[Azure]], [[Cod]], [[Salmon]] and [[Verdant]] are currently lacking [[Critters|Critter]] mob type.
|Name|Attribute|Capture Method|
|-|-|-|
|[[Azure]]|[[Azure Shard]] Forest Strength ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]).|
|[[Verdant]]|[[Verdant Shard]] Forest Fishing ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]).|
|[[Cod]]|[[Cod Shard]] Cheapskate ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]). [[Cod]] hit by a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]) gain a Spin meter. Reaching 90% Spin causes it to faint and be captured. If Spin exceeds 100%, it explodes and awards nothing.|
|[[Salmon]]|[[Salmon Shard]] Lost and Found ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]). [[Salmon]] hit by a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]) slow down and gain a ⁂ symbol above its head. After reaching 4 ⁂ symbols, it becomes exhausted and captured. The amount of symbols gained is based on the player's [[Pull]].|
|[[Coralot]]|[[Coralot Shard]] Bucket Lover ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]). Coralots ([[Coralot]]) become startled when a player is near, causing a warning to appear above their head. The player needs to stand still for 8s before capturing them. Throwing a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]) before 8s causes the [[Coralot]] to leave without a reward.|
|[[Joydive]]|[[Joydive Shard]] Deep Diving ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a [[Medium Fishing Net]] or better. Upon being hit with a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]), Joydives ([[Joydive]]) swim into a small cave in the [[Murkwater Depths]]. Once inside, throwing a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]) deals damage.|
|[[Lumisquid]]|[[Lumisquid Shard]] Extreme Pressure ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a [[Medium Fishing Net]] or better. Upon being hit with a Fishing Net ([[Fishing Nets]]), Lumisquids ([[Lumisquid]]) apply Blindness ([[Blindness Potion]]) for 2 seconds, teleport to a nearby location, and shrink in size.|
|[[Shellwise]]|[[Shellwise Shard]] Shell ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a [[Turbo Fishing Net]]. The Attribute Shards ([[Attributes]]) gained depend on its maximum health. 1,000 [[Health]] grants 1, 1,500 [[Health]] grants 2, and 2,000 [[Health]] grants 3.|
|[[Spike]]|[[Spike Shard]] Payback ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a [[Turbo Fishing Net]]. Spikes ([[Spike]]) can only be captured from 10 blocks away.|

|[[Phanpyre]]|[[Phanpyre Shard]] Nocturnal Animal ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a Lasso ([[Lassos]]). Phanpyres ([[Phanpyre]]) have a chance to spawn when chopping a tree on the [[Moonglade Marsh]]. They have 1 stamina bar. The chance to spawn a [[Phanpyre]] can be increased with Tree Lurker ([[Attributes]]).|

Phanflares ([[Phanflare]]) have a chance to spawn when chopping a tree on the [[Moonglade Marsh]]. They have 2 stamina bars. The chance to spawn a [[Phanflare]] can be increased with Tree Lurker ([[Attributes]]).|
|[[Dreadwing]]|[[Dreadwing Shard]] Essence of the Forest ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a Lasso ([[Lassos]]). It can also be hit with [[Axes]] and captured with Pocket Black Holes ([[Black Holes]]). Dreadwings ([[Dreadwing]]) have a chance to spawn when chopping a tree on the [[Moonglade Marsh]]. They have 5 stamina bars. The chance to spawn a [[Dreadwing]] can be increased with Tree Lurker ([[Attributes]]).|
|[[Invisibug]]|[[Invisibug Shard]] Visitor Bait ([[Attributes]])|Capture with a Lasso ([[Lassos]]).|

# Damage Calculation
Aliases: Boss Damage Cap, Boss damage cap, Combat Mechanics, Damage Cap, Damage Formula, Drowning Damage, Explosion Damage, Fall Damage, Final Damage, Fire Damage, Intelligence Scaling, Lava Damage, Magic Damage, Magic Resistance, Melee Damage, Poison Damage, Projectile Damage, Void Damage, Wither Damage
Categories: Confirmations needed, Player Statistics, Article stubs, Outdated pages, Mechanics, Pages with math equations
[Final Damage]
Final Damage Stat Icon.png This stat has not seen an in-game icon. This icon is chosen for wiki use.
Type: Stat
The [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] output of a player depends on various stats and factors. Calculating [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] is explained below.
## Terminology
[[Damage]] and [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] are distinct concepts.
[[Damage]] refers to the non-displayed player statistic which is mostly given by the held [[Weapons|weapon]] (cf. Damage: +100 [[Aspect of the End]] ).
  [[Damage]] is a statistic used to calculate [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]].
[[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] is not a player statistic.
  It is the damage a player applies on enemy [[Health]] by damaging it.
  [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] is commonly confused with the [[Damage]] statistic as it is also called damage.
The statistics and values which are used to calculate [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] can be separated into 3 broad categories:
  #Sword and Bow Damage - [[Damage Calculation|Melee Damage]] ([[Swords]]) or [[Damage Calculation|Projectile Damage]] ([[Bows]])
  #Ability Damage - [[Damage Calculation|Magic Damage]] (#Damage Abilities)
  #Damage Multipliers - Miscellaneous buffs or mechanics #Additive Multipliers - A common buff category (one example: [[Sharpness]]) #Multiplicative Multipliers - An uncommon buff category (one example: [[Book of Progression|Extra Infliction]]) #Defense Damage Reduction - [[Damage Reduction]] from [[Defense]] and/or [[True Defense]]. #Special Damage Reduction - Other forms of [[Damage Reduction]].
## Damage Types
Damage comes in a variety of forms:
|Type|Description|Notes|Effective Enchants (Reducing Damage, Directly)|
|-|-|-|-|
|Melee Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage received from a mob standing within close proximity .; Or Damage dealt by the Player with a Sword ([[Swords]]).|Decreased by [[Defense]].|[[Protection]]|
|Projectile Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage received from a mob using a bow or other projectile weapon.; Or Damage dealt by the Player with a Bow ([[Bows]]).|Decreased by [[Defense]].|[[Projectile Protection]]|
|Fall Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage received when falling from a great height.|Can be decreased with [[Feather Falling]], [[Feather Talisman]], and its upgrades.|[[Feather Falling]]|
|Drowning Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage received when staying underwater for too long.|Can be delayed and/or prevented with [[Respiration]] and [[Pressure Resistance]]. While in the [[Rift Dimension]], [[Rift Time]] depletes 2x faster instead of losing [[Hearts]].||
|Fire Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage received when on fire.; Or damage dealt by 'fire-based' abilities ([[Fire Aspect]]).||[[Fire Protection]]|
|Lava Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage received when in lava.||[[Fire Protection]]|
|Magic Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Ability Damage dealt by players or certain types of mobs( e.g. Terracotta, Ghosts).|Decreased by [[Defense]].||
|Void Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage received when in the void, found beneath all islands.|||
|Poison Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage dealt by the [[Poison]] effect and related damage abilities ([[Venomous]]).|Can be decreased by the [[Vaccine Talisman]].||
|Wither Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage dealt by the Wither effect and related damage abilities (e.g. Death's Touch) ([[Wither Skeleton Pet]]).|Can be negated with the [[Obsidian Chestplate]].||
|Explosion Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|Damage received when in the radius of an explosion.; Or damage dealt by the Player with an explosion-based ability (e.g. Implosion ([[Necron's Blade Scrolls]])).|Can be negated with the [[Creeper Hat]].|[[Blast Protection]]|
### Damage Indicator
The damage indicator is a feature that displays how much damage has been dealt to a mob. It also displays some information about the damage instance by changing appearance, for example if a hit is a [[Crit Chance]] critical hit.
|Condition|Display|
|-|-|
|Non-critical hit|123|
|[[Crit Chance]] Critical hit|✧123,456✧|
|[[Fire Aspect]] Fire Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|123,456|
|[[Venomous]] Poison Damage ([[Damage Calculation]])|123,456|
|[[Tarantula Armor]] and [[Primordial Armor]] Octodexterity bonus activates|Unarmed critical hit: ✧123,456✧+; Non-critical hit: 123,456+; Weapon critical hit: ✧123,456✧+|
|[[Ring of Love]] or its upgrades proc|The Damage Indicator will append a pink heart: ❤|
## Important Notes
- Calculated damage is rounded down to the nearest integer.
- Many values needed for calculations are not displayed to the player, requiring manual tracking of sources.
- In-game descriptions do not follow consistent rules; verification of the actual implementation of mechanics is required to calculate values correctly.
  Refer to the sections #Multiplicative Sources, #Additive Sources and #Special Mechanics documenting the actual implementation of items and mechanics.
  Some examples of this lack of consistency: [[Halberd of the Shredded]] +250% damage against [[Undead (Mob Type)|Undead]] enemies is a Multiplicative Multiplier of 3.5× damage. [[Golden Dragon Pet]] Perk Legendary Treasure (Up to) 250% damage gain is an Additive buff of up to +250 Additive. [[Smite]] enchantment +40% damage dealt increase is an Additive buff of +40 Additive. [[Archer]] Class 280% Arrow Damage is a Multiplicative Multiplier of 2.8× damage. [[Archery Potion|Archery]] 75% Bow Damage increase is an Additive buff of +75 Additive. [[Red Scarf]] Loving Bonus 5% ability damage increase is a Multiplicative Multiplier of 1.05×. [[Ender Dragon Pet]] Perk End Strike letting the player deal +200% damage against [[Ender]] enemies is an Additive buff of +200 Additive. [[Hedgehog Pet]] Perk Spiky Quills letting the player deal +100% damage against [[Pest (Mob Type)|Pest]]s is a Multiplicative Multiplier of 2×.
## Sword and Bow Damage
The following 3 statistics are the primary factors which increase [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Melee Damage]] ([[Swords]]) or [[Damage Calculation|Projectile Damage]] ([[Bows]]).
- [[Damage]] - the actual [[Damage]] statistic used to calculate [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Melee Damage]] ([[Swords]]) or [[Damage Calculation|Projectile Damage]] ([[Bows]]).
  [[Damage]] is also called Weapon Damage or Base Weapon Damage to reduce confusion with other uses of the word damage.
- [[Crit Damage]] - a displayed statistic used to calculate [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]]. It is only used if a hit successfully crits.
  Landing a critical hit is determined by [[Crit Chance]] and miscellaneous other conditions.
- [[Strength]] - a displayed statistic which is used to calculate [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] similarly to [[Crit Damage]].
  It is always used when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Melee Damage]] ([[Swords]]) or [[Damage Calculation|Projectile Damage]] ([[Bows]])
They are used to calculate the following multipliers:
- Weapon Damage Multiplier=(5+WeaponDamage)
- Strength Multiplier=(1+Strength100)
- Crit Damage Multiplier=(1+CritDamage100)
On a critical hit, when no other #Damage Multipliers (e.g. [[Defense]] ) exist, [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] is equal to the following: InitialDamagemelee or bow=Weapon Damage Multiplier×Strength Multiplier×Crit Damage Multiplier=(5+WeaponDamage)×(1+Strength100)×(1+CritDamage100)
## Ability Damage
The following 4 values are the primary factors which increase [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Magic Damage]] using Abilities such as [[Necron's Blade Scrolls]] .
- Base Ability Damage - a non-displayed value which increases [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Magic Damage]] (Abilities).
  Every Ability has its own Base Ability Damage, which can be increased by the player's total [[Catacombs|Catacombs Stat Bonus]] while in [[Catacombs|The Catacombs]]. Example: [[Necron's Blade Scrolls|Implosion]] and [[Necron's Blade Scrolls|Wither Impact]] have a Base Ability Damage of 10000. If the player is in the Catacombs with a total Stat Boost of +400%, Base Ability Damage = 10000 × (100% + 400%) = 50000
- Ability Scaling - a non-displayed value which determines the effectiveness of [[Intelligence]] when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Magic Damage]] (Abilities).
  Every Ability has its own Ability Scaling which cannot be increased. Example: [[Necron's Blade Scrolls|Implosion]] and [[Necron's Blade Scrolls|Wither Impact]] have an Ability Scaling factor of 0.3.
- [[Intelligence]] - a displayed statistic used to calculate [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Magic Damage]] (Abilities).
- [[Ability Damage]] - a displayed statistic used to calculate [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Magic Damage]] (Abilities).
  [[Ability Damage]] is also called Bonus Ability Damage to reduce confusion with other uses of the term ability damage.
They are used to calculate the following multipliers:
- Intelligence Multiplier=(1+Intelligence100×AbilityScaling)
- Bonus Ability Damage Multiplier=(1+Bonus Ability Damage100)
When no other #Damage Multipliers (e.g. [[Defense]] ) exist, [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] is equal to the following: InitialDamageability=BaseAbilityDamage×Intelligence Multiplier×Bonus Ability Damage Multiplier=BaseAbilityDamage×(1+Intelligence100×AbilityScaling)×(1+BonusAbilityDamage100)

## Damage Multipliers
The Damage Multipliers which increase [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] fall into 2 main categories
  Additive Multipliers (short: additive) A collection of non-displayed statistics and mechanics which increase [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]].
  Multiplicative Multipliers (short: multiplicative) A collection of non-displayed statistics and mechanics which increase [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]].
The Damage multipliers which decrease [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]]
  [[Defense]] Damage Reduction Damage Reduction from [[Defense]] or [[True Defense]]
  Special-Damage Reduction Special mechanics such as the [[Bosses|BOSS]] damage cap or Magic-Damage Reduction
### Additive Multipliers
Additive buffs add up to a total number, similarly to displayed statistics ( e.g. [[Strength]] ). The total Additive buff is then used as a multiplier in calculating [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]]. The Additive Multiplier is calculated by taking the sum (∑) of all Additive sources and dividing them by 100, as they are all provided in percent format (%=1100):
  Additive Multiplier=(1+∑Additive Buffs100)
Additive Example
Scenario:
1. 100 Additive from [[Prosecute|Prosecute VI]] on a full [[Health]] target.
2. 250 Additive from the [[Golden Dragon Pet]] Legendary Treasure perk, at Level 200 with 1,000,000,000 [[Coins]] in the bank.
3. The #Sword and Bow Damage calculation results in InitialDamagemelee=100.
Calculation: Additive Multiplier=(1+∑Additive Buffs100)=(1+100+250100)=(1+3.5)=4.5 Applying the Additive Multiplier: Damagepost-additive=InitialDamagemelee×Additive Multiplier=100×4.5=450

Ruler Attributes give +3 Additive Damage per [[Attributes|Attribute]] level, up to +30 Additive Damage. Ruler Attributes are buffed by [[Caiman Shard]] Attribute Echo of Ruler and [[Tiamat Shard]] Attribute Echo of Echoes. ### Multiplicative Multipliers
Multiplicative buffs multiply with each-other, resulting in the product (∏) of all Multiplicative buffs multiplying [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]]:
  Multiplicative Multiplier=∏Multiplicative Buffs
Multiplicative Example
Scenario:
- 1.15× multiplicative from [[Crown of Avarice]] ability Overindulgence with 1,000,000,000 [[Coins]] consumed.
- 3.5× multiplicative (against [[Undead (Mob Type)|Undead]] enemies) from [[Halberd of the Shredded]].
  - The enemy is [[Undead (Mob Type)|Undead]].
- The #Sword and Bow Damage calculation results in InitialDamagemelee=100.
Calculation: Multiplicative Multiplier=∏Multiplicative Buffs=1.15×3.5=4.025 Applying the Multiplicative Multiplier: Damagepost-multiplicative=InitialDamagemelee×Multiplicative Multiplier=100×4.025=402.5

### Defense Damage Reduction
Damage Reduction reduces the [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] enemies take. [[Defense]] and [[True Defense]] both use the same formula to calculate how much damage reduction is granted based on their value: Damage Reduction=Defense100+Defense Converting damage reduction into a damage taken-multiplier: multiplierdamage-taken=1−Damage Reduction This results in the following damage taken-multiplier for [[Defense]] and [[True Defense]]: multdef−dmg taken=100100+Defense Shorten that to multdef.
[Lv40] [[Tank Zombie]] [[Defense]] Example
Scenario:
- The [Lv40] [[Tank Zombie]] has 2000 [[Defense]].
- The #Sword and Bow Damage calculation results in InitialDamagemelee=1000.
Calculation: multdef=100100+Defense=100100+2000=121≈4.8% Applying the damage taken-multiplier: Damagepost-def=InitialDamagemelee×multdef=1000×121=100021≈47.62
### Special Damage Reduction
Damage Reduction reduces the [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] enemies take. Special mechanics can provide damage reduction which applies in the same way as [[Defense]] damage reduction. If multiple sources of damage reduction exist, the damage taken-multipliers of the respective damage reductions all apply in a specific order:
[[Scarf]] special damage reduction and magic damage reduction Example
Scenario:
1. This hypothetical version of [[Scarf]] has a magic resistance of 15% (the real [[Scarf]] does not!). That results in a damage taken-multiplier of: multmagicDR=1−15%=0.85
2. [[Scarf]] has a damage cap threshold of 6000 [[Health]] .
3. [[Scarf]] has 0 [[Defense]].
4. [[Scarf]] and Master [[Scarf]] have a special mechanic: Based on the amount of [[Scarf|Undead Minions]] alive, the following damage taken-multiplier is applied: multspecial=max⁡((1−0.24×UndeadsAlive),0) 3 [[Scarf|Undead Minions]] are alive, giving it the following value: multspecial=max⁡(1−0.24×3,0)=0.28
5. The damager uses a [[Book of Progression]] set to Extra Infliction at [[SkyBlock Levels|SkyBlock Level]][370]. This results in a Multiplicative Multiplier of multExtraInfliction=1.037
6. The #Ability Damage calculation results in InitialDamageability=7000.
Calculation: The order of applying these specific multipliers is the following:
1. Magic Resistance
2. damage cap
3. [[Defense]]
4. Special Damage Reduction
5. Extra Infliction
Applying the multipliers:
1. Magic Resistance: Damagepost-magicDR=multmagicDR×InitialDamageability=0.85×7000=5950
2. Damage is below the damage cap threshold of 6000 [[Health]] : Damagepost-damageCap=Damagepost-magicDR
3. 0 [[Defense]] results in no damage reduction: Damagepost-def=Damagepost-damageCap
4. Special damage reduction: Damagepost-special=multspecial×Damagepost-def=0.28×5950=1666
5. Extra Infliction: Damagepost-ExtraInfliction=multExtraInfliction×Damagepost-special=1.037×1666≈1727.64
The unique damage cap damage reduction functionality is very sensitive to the ordering of how damage is calculated:
[[Apex Dragon]] [[Bosses|[[Bosses|BOSS]]]] damage cap and [[Defense]] damage reduction Example
[[Bosses|[[Bosses|BOSS]]]] [[Damage Calculation|damage cap]] is a damage reduction but it only applies above thresholds, meaning the order of damage reduction application matters: [[Bosses|BOSS]] enemies have a damage cap which reduces the [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] they take above a defined damage cap threshold. If damage exceeds this threshold, the excess is multiplied by 0.1. Scenario:
1. The [[Apex Dragon]] has a damage cap threshold of 100k [[Health]] .
2. The [[Apex Dragon]] has 2500 [[Defense]].
3. The #Sword and Bow Damage calculation results in InitialDamagebow=600000000.
Calculation: The damage is above the damage cap threshold: Damagepost-damageCap=0.1×(InitialDamagebow−damageCap)+damageCap=0.1×(600000000−100000)+100000=60090000 Damagepost-def=Damagepost-damageCap×multdef=60090000×100100+2500≈2311153.85 Were the multiplier application order reversed, the result would be:
  0.1×(60000000026−100000)+100000≈2397692.31
When damage exceeds the damage cap threshold by a significant amount, the order does not have strong impact:
  2397692.312311153.85≈1.037
However, when damage does not exceed the threshold significantly, the order of applying [[Defense]] can have more significant impact. When #Sword and Bow Damage calculation results in InitialDamagebow=3000000, the impact of calculation order is significant:
  (300000026−100000)×0.1+100000(3000000−100000)×0.1×126≈6.77
### Multiplier interactions
Many multipliers combine intuitively, for example Additive Multiplier and Multiplicative Multiplier combining:
Example 1: Additive and Multiplicative buffs combining
The scenarios from the #Additive Multipliers Example and #Multiplicative Multipliers Example combine:
1. Multiplicative Multiplier=4.025
2. Additive Multiplier=4.5
3. The #Sword and Bow Damage calculation results in InitialDamagemelee=100.
Calculation: Damagepost-mults=InitialDamagemelee×Multiplicative Multiplier×Additive Multiplier=100×4.025×4.5=1811.25
Multipliers can also interact in unique ways, resulting in unexpected outcomes - especially due to ordering. As seen in the Apex Dragon Example, when applying the [[Bosses|[[Bosses|BOSS]]]] damage cap, calculation order is very important:
  The Additive Multiplier applies before the damage cap, but not every Multiplicative Multiplier does:
  As seen in the Scarf Example, Extra Infliction applies after the damage cap.
But special mechanics can lead to unexpected interactions even with the Additive Multiplier:
Example 2: [[Barbarian Duke X]] Flame Domain and [[Bosses|[[Bosses|BOSS]]]] damage cap
The Flame Domain ability of the [[Barbarian Duke X]] modifies the Additive Multiplier by setting it to 0.8. But this does not make every additive buff ineffective because the modifier applies inbetween additive buffs:
  Some additive buffs, especially [[Enchantments]] like [[Prosecute]] and [[Giant Killer]] are rendered ineffective, never increasing [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]].
  Other additive buffs, such as the [[Combat]] ability Warrior and [[Golden Dragon Pet]] perk Legendary Treasure add to the final Additive Multiplier, being just as effective as before. It means they actually have a higher relative impact than in usual circumstances.
[[Bosses|BOSS]] enemies have a damage cap which reduces the damage they take above a defined damage cap threshold. If damage exceeds this threshold, the excess is multiplied by 0.1.
  The damage cap threshold of [[Barbarian Duke X]] is 1.6% Max [[Health]] ( = 1,120,000 [[Health]] ).
Scenario:
1. [[Prosecute|Prosecute VI]] and [[Giant Killer|Giant Killer VII]] are used with [[Barbarian Duke X]] on full [[Health]]. Resulting in [[Prosecute|Prosecute VI]] adding +100 additive and [[Giant Killer|Giant Killer VII]] adding +65 additive.
2. [[Combat|Combat LX (60)]] ability Warrior LX and [[Golden Dragon Pet]] perk Legendary Treasure with 1,000,000,000 [[Coins]] in the [[Bank]]. Warrior LX adds +210 additive and Legendary Treasure adds +250 additive.
3. The #Ability Damage calculation results in InitialDamageability=200000 for [[Necron's Blade Scrolls|Implosion]].
4. The Multiplicative Multiplier has a value of 1.
Calculation:
1. The Additive Multiplier is updated by the [[Enchantments]], adding +165 additive for a total Additive Multiplier of 2.65.
2. The Additive Multiplier is updated by Flame Domain, setting it to 0.8.
3. The Additive Multiplier is updated by Warrior LX and Legendary Treasure, adding to it +460 additive for a total Additive Multiplier of 5.4. The final Additive Multiplier is applied as a multiplicative buff of (1+−20+210+250100)=(1+4.4)=5.4.
4. Applying Additive Multiplier and Multiplicative Multiplier: Damagepost-mults=InitialDamageability×Additive Multiplier×Multiplicative Multiplier=200000×5.4×1=1080000
5. The damage is below the damagecap threshold, so the result is 1,080,000 [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]].
Equipping an [[Implosion Belt]] offers 1.25 multiplicative from its ability Consolidated, resulting in the following calculation:
  Damagepost-mults=InitialDamageability×Additive Multiplier×Multiplicative Multiplier=200000×5.4×1.25=1350000
  The damage is above the damage cap threshold, requiring it to be applied:
  Damagepost-damageCap=0.1×(Damagepost-mults−damageCap)+damageCap=0.1×(230000)+1120000=1143000
Adding the Consolidated Ability resulted in 1,143,000 [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] - a relative damage increase of 11430001080000≈1.0583 - lower than expected from the 1.25 multiplicative due to the damage cap.
Damage over time (DoT) effect calculations are some of the more complicated:
Example 3: [[Apex Dragon]] [[Toxic Arrow Poison]]
This uses the Apex Dragon Example scenario. Scenario:
1. An arrow damages the [[Apex Dragon]]. The #Sword and Bow Damage calculation for the arrow results in InitialDamagebow=600000000.
2. [[Toxic Arrow Poison]] is used and activates. [[Toxic Arrow Poison]] deals 0.025× damage every second for 4 seconds.
3. The [[Apex Dragon]] has a Magic Resistance of 75% resulting in taking 0.25× damage from [[Toxic Arrow Poison]].
4. The [[Apex Dragon]] has a damage cap threshold of 100k [[Health]] .
5. The [[Apex Dragon]] has 2500 [[Defense]].
Calculation:
1. [[Toxic Arrow Poison]] damage calculation: Damagepoison=InitialDamagebow×0.025=600000000×0.025=15000000
2. Applying Magic Resistance: Damagepost−magicDR=Damagepoison×(1−75%)=15000000×0.25=3750000
3. Applying the damage cap: Damagepost−damageCap=(Damagepost−magicDR−100000)×0.1+100000=(3750000−100000)×0.1+100000=465000
4. Applying [[Defense]]: Damagepost−def=Damagepost−damageCap×100100+2500=465000×126≈17884.62
This means [[Toxic Arrow Poison]] would deal about 17,844.62 [[Health]] every second for 4 seconds. In total that would be 4×17844.622311153.85≈3.1% of the arrow damage calculated in the Special Damage Reduction Apex Dragon Example - lower than the expected 10%.
While the case of [[Toxic Arrow Poison]] DoT calculation follows expected logic, some other DoT damagers do not:
Example 4: [[Fire Aspect]] and [[Venomous]]
Scenario:
1. A [[Swords|Sword]] enchanted with [[Fire Aspect|Fire Aspect II]]and [[Venomous|Venomous III]] is used to damage a [[Training Dummy]]. [[Fire Aspect|Fire Aspect II]] damages for 0.06× damage. [[Venomous|Venomous III]] damages for 0.006× damage per stack. The amount of stacks is the maximum (41).
2. The #Sword and Bow Damage calculation results in InitialDamagemelee=2000.
3. The only source increasing Additive Multiplier is Warrior LX. Additive Multiplier=1+210100=3.1
Calculation:
1. Calculating the damage with additive applied: Damagepost−additive=InitialDamagemelee×Additive Multiplier=2000×3.1=6200
2. Calculating [[Fire Aspect|Fire Aspect II]] DoT damage: DamagefireAspect=Damagepost−additive×0.06=6200×0.06=372
3. Calculating [[Venomous|Venomous III]] DoT damage: Damagevenomous=Damagepost−additive×0.006×stacksvenomous×Additive Multiplier=6200×0.006×41×3.1=115.32×41=4728.12
So yes, Venomous applies the Additive Multiplier another time, while Fire Aspect does not. But it actually does not apply the same Additive Multiplier as the original damage - instead it calculates the Additive Multiplier again. This can be seen when adding [[Sharpness|Sharpness V]]. Sharpness Calculation:
1. Calculating the damage with additive applied: Damagepost−additive=InitialDamagemelee×Additive Multipliermelee=2000×(1+210+30100)=2000×3.4=6800
2. [[Venomous]] damage is not melee damage so [[Sharpness]] is not added: Additive Multipliervenomous=(1+210100)=3.1
3. Calculating [[Venomous|Venomous III]] DoT damage: Damagevenomous=Damagepost−additive×0.006×stacksvenomous×Additive Multipliervenomous=6800×0.006×41×3.1=126.48×41=5158.68
DoT/debuff effects have another quirk which is very important when calculating their damage:
  Values such as [[Enchantments|enchantment level]] and damage are saved on the applying hit and are saved and used for the duration of the debuff.
  For an enchantment such as [[Lethality]], it means hitting an enemy until max [[Lethality]] stacks with [[Lethality|Lethality I]] will reduce the enemy [[Defense]] by 4.8%. Hitting that enemy with [[Lethality|Lethality II]] or higher will not increase that 4.8% - instead it will simply refresh the debuff duration, keeping the -4.8% in place, even though [[Lethality|Lethality VI]] could debuff enemy [[Defense]] by 9% per hit up to -36%. Only when the duration expires, a new instance of the debuff (with a different level!) can be applied.
This makes not having lower levels of debuff enchantments on [[Swords]] importants, as it may decrease damage:
Example 5: DoT snapshotting: [[Fire Aspect]] and [[Venomous]]
[[Fire Aspect]] also follows the rule of updating its duration. Dealing 100,000 [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] with a [[Giant's Sword]] and applying the [[Fire Aspect|Fire Aspect III]] DoT with that hit would set the fire damage to: 100000×0.09=9000 Then swapping to a [[Fire Aspect|Fire Aspect II]] [[Aspect of the Jerry]] will refresh the timer of the 9,000 damage DoT, without decreasing it. Using [[Ragnarock]] to increase damage temporarily to 150,000 [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] with the [[Giant's Sword]] will update the value of the DoT to: 150000×0.09=13500 [[Venomous]] however functions a little differently:
1. The damage of every [[Venomous]] stack (before applying the second Additive Multiplier) is saved together.
2. The total saved [[Venomous]] damage cannot be increased further once 41 stacks have been saved.
3. The second Additive Multiplier is re-calculated on every instance of the DoT damaging. This means even fully stacked [[Venomous]] damage can change somewhat due to the additive sources used for the second Additive Multiplier changing.
Scenario:
- A [[Giant's Sword]] with [[Venomous|Venomous V]] deals 100,000 [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] per hit.
- A [[Aspect of the Jerry]] with [[Venomous|Venomous I]] deals 1,000 [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] per hit.
- The enemy is [[Undead (Mob Type)|Undead]] and the [[Aspect of the Jerry]] is enchanted with [[Smite|Smite VI]].
- The Additive Multiplier is 5.6 before [[Enchantments|Sword Enchantments]].
Based on this scenario, many [[Venomous]] damages can result. In this example, 3 will be calculated:
1. Full [[Giant's Sword]] [[Venomous]] stacking.
2. Full [[Aspect of the Jerry]] [[Venomous]] stacking.
3. 15 [[Giant's Sword]] [[Venomous]] stacks and 26 [[Aspect of the Jerry]] [[Venomous]] stacks.
Calculation:
Damagevenomous=Damagepost−additive×0.012×stacksvenomous×Additive Multipliervenomous=100000×0.012×41×5.6=275520 Holding the [[Smite|Smite VI]] [[Aspect of the Jerry]] after setting the [[Venomous]] damage value would increase the second Additive Multiplier to 6.0, resulting in the following damage: Damagevenomous=100000×0.012×41×6=295200
Damagevenomous=Damagepost−additive×0.002×stacksvenomous×Additive Multipliervenomous=1000×0.002×41×6.0=492 Hitting with the [[Giant's Sword]] after setting the [[Venomous]] damage value would not only fail to increase the DoT damage - but actually decrease the second Additive Multiplier to 5.6, resulting in the following damage: Damagevenomous=1000×0.002×41×5.6=459.2
Damagevenomous=(DamageGiant's×0.012×stacksGiant's+DamageAotJ×0.002×stacksAotJ)×Additive Multipliervenomous=(100000×0.012×15+1000×0.002×26)×Additive Multipliervenomous=18052×Additive Multipliervenomous Resulting in: Damagevenomous={AotJ held:18052×6.0=108312Giant's held:18052×5.6=101091.2
### Special Mechanics
Many special mechanics function in unique ways which are documented here:
#### "Add Damage" type mechanic
"Add-Damage" type mechanics add damage to the [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] which is only affected by the Crit Damage Multiplier.
|Name|Description|Implementation|
|-|-|-|
|Soul Eater I–V ([[Soul Eater]])|Your weapon gains 2-10x the Mob [[Damage]] of the latest monster killed and adds it as [[Strength]] on your next critical hit.|[[Soul Eater]] damage is affected by the Crit Damage Multiplier but not multipliers such as the Additive Multiplier:; Damagepost−multipliers=; (Weapon Damage Multiplier×Strength Multiplier×multipliers+multSoulEater×DamageMobSoul)×Crit Damage Multiplier|
|One Punch I-V Dragon Essence Shop ([[Essence Shops]]) perk|After all other damage, add 500-2500 damage to the first strike against Endermen ([[Enderman]]) and Endermites ([[Endermite]]).|One Punch damage is affected by the Crit Damage Multiplier but not multipliers such as the Additive Multiplier:; Damagepost−multipliers=; (Weapon Damage Multiplier×Strength Multiplier×multipliers+500×levelOnePunch)×Crit Damage Multiplier|
|[[End Stone Bow]] ability Extreme focus|Consumes all your [[Mana]], and your next hit will deal that much more Damage!|Extreme focus damage is affected by the Crit Damage Multiplier but not multipliers such as the Additive Multiplier:[Confirm]; Damagepost−multipliers=; (Weapon Damage Multiplier×Strength Multiplier×multipliers+DamageExtreme focus)×Crit Damage Multiplier|
#### Magic Resistance
Magic resistance reduces damage taken from [[Damage Calculation|Magic Damage]]. If a mob has a magic resistance of x%, that results in a damage taken-multiplier of: multmagicDR=1−x% Magic resistance is common on [[Catacombs Mobs|Dungeon Mobs]].
This section needs more information. Please help expand it.

|Name|Magic Resistance|Note|
|-|-|-|
|[[Apex Dragon]]|75%||
|Chaos Guardian ([[The Professor]])|80%|[Confirm]|
|Melee Dungeon Mobs ([[Catacombs Mobs]])|10%|[Confirm]|
|Ranged Dungeon Mobs ([[Catacombs Mobs]])|30%|[Confirm]|
|Magic-type Dungeon Mobs ([[Catacombs Mobs]]) (e.g. [[Withermancer]])|90%|[Confirm]|
|[[Necron]]|99%|[Confirm]|
#### Damage Cap
Some enemies ( usually [[Bosses|BOSS]] mobs ) have a damage cap which reduces the damage they take above a defined damage cap threshold. If damage exceeds this threshold, the excess is multiplied by the excess multiplier which has a value 0.1 in most cases:
  Damagepost-damageCap=0.1×(Damagepre-damageCap−damageCap)+damageCap If damage does not exceed the threshold, damage will not be adjusted.
Mobs can have more than one damage cap. In such cases, the damage caps are applied in order of lowest threshold first, then ascending.
This section needs more information. Please help expand it.
See more stubs.
|Name|Max Health|Damage Cap threshold(s)|Note(s) (If the Damage Cap Excess Multiplier differs from 0.1 or more than 1 Damage Cap exists, it will be noted here.)|
|-|-|-|-|
|[[Barbarian Duke X]]|70,000,000 [[Health]]|1. 1,120,000 [[Health]]; 2. 1,680,000 [[Health]]|Multiple damage caps|
|[[Mage Outlaw]]|70,000,000 [[Health]]|1. 1,120,000 [[Health]]; 2. 1,680,000 [[Health]]; 3. 1,712,800 [[Health]]|Multiple damage caps; Damage cap #3 uses excess multiplier = 0|
|[[Bladesoul]]|50,000,000 [[Health]]|1. 800,000 [[Health]]; 2. 1,200,000 [[Health]]|Multiple damage caps|
|[[Apex Dragon]]|1,000,000,000 [[Health]]|100,000 [[Health]]||
|[[Power Dragon]]|1,000,000,000 [[Health]]|100,000 [[Health]]||
|[[Flame Dragon]]|1,000,000,000 [[Health]]|100,000 [[Health]]||

### Ghost Damage
Ghost Damage is a phenomenon of [[Damage Calculation|Final Damage]] not being subtracted from enemy [[Health]], even though the enemy is not immune to a damage type and the damage indicator may even indicate the damage value. This happens when dealing [[Damage Calculation|Magic Damage]] to [[Ender Dragon|Ender Dragons]] and when [[Ferocity]] activates on bosses which display a leaderboard upon death. All Ghost Damage dealt will still count towards the player's leaderboard position.

This is an advanced damage calculator with more features. There are custom fields. If one of those fields was empty at the beginning, leave them empty once you do not use them. Hold an item and check the stats in-game. Not all bonus stats get added together on the profile stats in the /sbmenu. There can be invisible stats, like Extra Bonus on Tarantula Helmet. Please note that the calculator will allow enchantment combinations that wouldn't work in-game, e.g. you wouldn't be able to have [[Execute]] and [[Prosecute]] together. Also note that arrows add additional damage as well. Note: All inputs must be non-negative integers under 1,000,000.
